# poLight and its competitors on the road to top-tier AR/VR/XR glasses — timeline map and commentary

*As of 2026-10-06. Interactive map: `timeline-map.html` (open locally or via the published artifact). Data: `timeline_data.json`. Every dated row carries its source URL; confidence is confirmed (primary source), strong (several reputable outlets), circumstantial (inference) or rumor (forum / unnamed sources).*

## 1. Headline findings

- Meta VR Glasses (spring 2027) is the only announced device with documented autofocus passthrough (2026-09-23); every supplier '2027' signal in the file converges on it, yet no primary source names the actuator supplier.
- Program A's three qualification POs (2025-08-06, 2026-02-11, 2026-06-18; ~NOK 2.5m in total) fall 18.8, 12.6 and 8.4 months before 2027-03-01: the first precedes the usual 9–18-month qualification band, the second sits inside it, and the third lands at the band's near edge where production purchase orders would normally take over. poLight still calls the program 'qualification' and has never said 2027; a production PO in the Sep 2026–Feb 2027 band is the missing confirmation.
- Program B became poLight's first consumer design-in on 2026-07-10 after four POs totalling NOK 4.9m over 228 days; with Q4 2026 deliveries and a 2026 launch, Samsung/Google (Nov 2026), Snap Specs and Xreal Aura (fall 2026) are the only matching camera-glasses windows, and none has disclosed its focus type; a Chinese ecosystem customer is also a live candidate, since poLight showed Ant Group 'Look to Pay' demo glasses with a TLens camera (Q2 2026 call) and Q Tech counts ten eyewear brands among its customers.
- Q Tech's wording moved from 'US M brand vendor code' (2024-08-12) to 'small-batch deliveries for 2 leading overseas terminal clients' (2026-03-16) to 'TLens + AR Mono Engine: Volume Delivery Achieved... Ramp in 2H26/2027' (2026-08-24): a 161-day step from small-batch to volume that fits the 3-6-month pre-launch ramp for a late-2026 or spring-2027 device.
- Meta's piezo tunable-lens patent stack (seven priorities Jan 2022-Aug 2023; grants 2025-12-09, 2026-05-19, 2026-06-23) cites poLight pages retrieved on 2022-10-19, and an ex-Meta engineer's profile says the module passed AR/VR/MR reliability qualification before Apr 2025: strong evidence of interest, but equally consistent with Meta having its own membrane lens built elsewhere.
- Meta's Reality Labs paper of 2025-04-17 argued against autofocus in all-day glasses; Ray-Ban Meta Gen 2 is documented fixed focus and no autofocus has been disclosed for Gen 3, Oakley Meta or Meta Ray-Ban Display, so TLens's 2027 Meta opportunity is the US$1,299.99 puck-powered VR Glasses, not the 7–10m-unit-per-year Ray-Ban line.
- Cambridge Mechatronics' XR effort peaked between 2021-10-18 and 2024-01-29 (SF office, three SPIE talks, CES 2023) and has gone quiet: absent from SPIE 2025 and 2026, last XR article 2025-05-12 (512 days ago), CTO Ben Brown to Apple in Mar 2026, and Meta abandoned its CML-citing wearable-camera application on 2025-05-08.
- Optotune's EL-3.1-10.8 (1.72 mm, 0.3 g, 20 mW at 10 dpt) launched 2026-09-21, 369 days after its handling instructions and two days before Connect 2026; it is the only non-poLight tunable lens with a glasses-class datasheet, but Optotune names no consumer customer and lists it for phones, action cams and robotics.
- Apple's N50 has slipped about 12 months across 492 days of reporting (end-2026 on 2025-05-23 to end-2027 on 2026-09-27) and is described with two cameras and no autofocus; the only Apple-side actuator signals are ex-CML CTO Ben Brown's move (Mar 2026) and SEMCO's fine-pitch-coil deal (2025-12-17), both pointing away from TLens.
- Among glasses that shipped in 2025-2026 every disclosed camera is fixed focus (Ray-Ban Meta Gen 2, Xreal Eye, Rokid Ai Glasses Style) or undisclosed (Snap Specs, Xreal Aura, Samsung SM-O200P, Alibaba N1 50 MP); the only teardown- or release-confirmed TLens XR wins remain Magic Leap 2 (2023-03-02), Vuzix Shield (2024-04-08) and SnkeXR (2025-10-31); the Ant Group demo glasses (2026-08-06) are a fourth, pre-product sighting.
- The DBS 'Meta + Q Tech + poLight, 1H27' sentence (2026-08-26) is timing-consistent with a spring-2027 launch (module mass production 2-4 months before), but it is unverified, absent from DBS's public summary and from DBS's own 2026-05-08 Meta note.
- The next hard checkpoints are poLight's Q3 report (2026-10-29) and Capital Markets Day (2026-10-30), Samsung's November launch, Snap and Xreal fall shipments, and poLight's Q4 report on 2027-02-24, five days before the VR Glasses window opens; a Program A production PO several times the ~NOK 2.5m of qualification orders to date would be the first non-circumstantial link.
- poLight reported no new PZT MEMS wafers ordered or delivered through 30 June 2026 (annual report 2025; Q2 2026 report); because wafer starts at STMicroelectronics precede volume assembly by roughly 6–9 months, a wafer order in Q3–Q4 2026 is a harder precondition for spring-2027 volumes than any purchase-order wording, and the Q3 report on 2026-10-29 is the first place it would surface.
- Open contradiction to watch: the Phoenix supply-chain preview names Sunny Optical for camera modules while the TLens trail runs through Q Tech; both are compatible with TLens only if poLight's 'support all camera-module partners' stance applies, and Sunny's own August 2026 deck lists a 'Compact High-Res AF' camera for AR glasses without naming the actuator.

## 2. How to read the map

- **Lanes**: the upper group is the glasses and headset producers, the lower group is poLight and every competing autofocus / tunable-lens supplier plus two evidence lanes (Meta patents & people; analysts, brokers & press).
- **Markers** are dated signals, coloured by category (product, commercial, IP & people, analyst/press). Hollow markers are circumstantial or rumour. A thin line under a marker shows the date's imprecision (month, quarter, half, year).
- **Bars** are launch windows or dated ranges; everything right of the today line is expected, not observed.
- **Washes** on supplier lanes show where supplier milestones are expected for a given future launch, working backwards with optics-industry lead times (see §4).
- **Match lines** connect a supplier signal to the launch it plausibly feeds; click a marker to read why, and what would contradict it.
- **Cadence strips** in the commentary show the days between a lane's updates.

## 4. Where supplier timelines meet future launches

### 4.1 Expected supplier milestone windows (lead-time logic)

**Meta VR Glasses retail launch — US$1,299.99, 25 countries, autofocus passthrough ("Spring 2027")** (2027-03-01 → 2027-05-31), drawn on lanes: poLight (TLens), Q Technology, Camera-module & assembly chain, VCM / SMA actuator makers, Optotune, Cambridge Mechatronics
- design-in / qualification complete: 2025-09-01 → 2026-05-31
- qualification orders expected: 2025-12-01 → 2026-08-31
- production POs / ramp expected: 2026-09-01 → 2027-02-28
- Rationale: Meta VR Glasses, US$1,299.99, 'Available Spring 2027' (2027-03-01 to 2027-05-31), the only announced device with documented 'passthrough with autofocus and depth'. Norms used: design-in / qualification complete 12-18 months before launch; qualification orders 9-15 months before; production POs and ramp 3-6 months before; module mass production 2-4 months before (Nov 2026-Mar 2027). Program A's qualification POs of 2025-08-06, 2026-02-11 and 2026-06-18 sit 18.8, 12.6 and 8.4 months before the window start, bracketing the 9-18-month qualification and design-in bands; Q Tech's 'Ramp in 2H26/2027' (2026-08-24) and Sunny Optical's 'volume ramp-up' (2026-08-26) sit in the ramp band. No production PO has been announced as of 2026-10-06 and Meta names no supplier; VCM, Optotune and CML are listed as alternatives Meta's own patents cite.

**"Artemis" consumer AR glasses (Orion successor) — 2027 per Bloomberg/Reuters; Bosworth non-committal** (2027-01-01 → 2027-12-31), drawn on lanes: poLight (TLens), Q Technology, Cambridge Mechatronics, Optotune, Camera-module & assembly chain, VCM / SMA actuator makers
- design-in / qualification complete: 2025-07-01 → 2026-12-31
- qualification orders expected: 2025-10-01 → 2027-03-31
- production POs / ramp expected: 2026-07-01 → 2027-09-30
- Rationale: Meta 'Artemis' consumer AR glasses, a full-year 2027 rumor window (Bloomberg/Reuters) that Bosworth 'would not commit to' on 2026-09-24, so the bands are wide. Same norms as above (12-18 / 9-15 / 3-6 months). Program C, the NOK 5m AR-camera design PO of 2025-10-13 with validation modules shipped end-Q2 2026, falls 14.6 and 6.1 months before 2027-01-01, which only fits design-in if Artemis launches late in 2027; poLight describes it as a camera 'that could be used in several AR head-worn devices', i.e. a platform. Meta's 2025-11-14 application for an LC/electrowetting 'tunable lens camera module for AR glasses' shows a non-piezo path still in research. Evidence for any supplier is thin.

**Quest 4 "Griffin" gaming headset — no earlier than H2 2027, possibly 2028** (2027-07-01 → 2028-12-31), drawn on lanes: Q Technology, Camera-module & assembly chain, VCM / SMA actuator makers, poLight (TLens)
- design-in / qualification complete: 2026-01-01 → 2027-12-31
- qualification orders expected: 2026-04-01 → 2028-03-31
- production POs / ramp expected: 2027-01-01 → 2028-09-30
- Rationale: Quest 4 'Griffin' gaming headset, no earlier than H2 2027 and possibly 2028 (rumor; Bosworth confirmed two devices on the roadmap on 2026-02-10). Quest 3 and 3S shipped 2x4 MP passthrough with no autofocus reported; if the VR Glasses' autofocus passthrough carries over, design-in would run Jan 2026-Dec 2027 under the 12-18-month norm. Q Tech's product map lists a 'VR VST TLens AF camera' and Luxshare is building a 9m-unit/yr Vietnam VR line tied to Meta. No direct evidence links any actuator supplier to Griffin.

**Meta Ray-Ban Display successor "Hypernova 2" (binocular) — timing unresolved H2 2026→2027** (2026-07-01 → 2027-12-31), drawn on lanes: poLight (TLens), Q Technology, Camera-module & assembly chain
- design-in / qualification complete: 2025-01-01 → 2026-12-31
- qualification orders expected: 2025-04-01 → 2027-03-31
- production POs / ramp expected: 2026-01-01 → 2027-09-30
- Rationale: Binocular Ray-Ban Display successor 'Hypernova 2', timing unresolved between H2 2026 (The Information, 2026-02-19) and 2027 (Bloomberg, 2025-04-01); not shown at Connect 2026 and Bosworth said on 2026-09-24 there is 'no new version of the hardware', so only the 2027 end of this rumor window is live. Bands follow the same 12-18 / 9-15 / 3-6-month norms. The current Display uses an OmniVision LCoS engine and a 12 MP camera with no autofocus claim; poLight's TWedge wobulation part is not expected to be mass-production ready before about 2028, so a 2027 Hypernova 2 would precede it. Evidence is thin on both display and camera actuators.

**Apple N50 AI glasses shipment late 2027 / "by the end of 2027"; 3-5M+ units in 2027 (Kuo)** (2027-10-01 → 2027-12-31), drawn on lanes: Cambridge Mechatronics, VCM / SMA actuator makers, Camera-module & assembly chain, Optotune, poLight (TLens)
- design-in / qualification complete: 2026-04-01 → 2026-12-31
- qualification orders expected: 2026-07-01 → 2027-03-31
- production POs / ramp expected: 2027-04-01 → 2027-09-30
- Rationale: Apple N50 display-less AI glasses, shipping 'by the end of 2027' with an unveil at WWDC June 2027 (Gurman 2026-07-26, reiterated 2026-09-27); two camera sensors, one capture and one computer-vision, with no autofocus or module vendor reported. Norms as above; Kuo's Q2 2027 mass-production call would pull production POs into Q1 2027. Apple-side actuator pointers: ex-CML CTO Ben Brown at Apple from about Mar 2026, 18 months before the window; SEMCO's fine-pitch coil supply to a 'global big tech' with possible XR expansion; LG Innotek's exclusive Vision Pro 3D-sensor role. Q Tech has no meaningful Apple exposure and poLight has never referred to Apple; Apple's own tunable-lens patents are prescription see-through lenses, not camera AF.

**Samsung intelligent eyewear SM-O200P / SM-O200J (display-less, single 12MP camera) launch November 2026** (2026-11-01 → 2026-11-30), drawn on lanes: poLight (TLens), Q Technology, VCM / SMA actuator makers, Camera-module & assembly chain
- design-in / qualification complete: 2025-05-01 → 2025-11-30
- qualification orders expected: 2025-08-01 → 2026-02-28
- production POs / ramp expected: 2026-05-01 → 2026-08-30
- Rationale: Samsung/Google intelligent eyewear SM-O200P (Warby Parker) / SM-O200J (Gentle Monster), November 2026 per a Samsung official (2026-10-01); FCC filing shows a single 12 MP camera on Snapdragon AR1 Gen 1. Autofocus is claimed only by VRARA 'early reports' (2026-02-09). Under the 12-18 / 9-15 / 3-6-month norms, design-in should have completed by Nov 2025 and production POs by Aug 2026. Program B's stream fits those bands loosely: the PoC PO of 2025-11-24 sits in the qualification band, the mass-production follow-ons of 2026-04-07 and 2026-05-20 and the 2026-07-10 design-in sit in the production-PO band, and Q4 2026 deliveries run 1-2 months later than the 2-4-month module-MP norm, which is compatible with a November launch only if the first build is small; Q Tech reported 'small-batch deliveries for 2 leading overseas terminal clients' at year-end 2025. Samsung's in-house actuator and module chain (SEMCO) is the default assumption; nothing names poLight or Q Tech.

**Android XR display AI glasses (in-lens display) in 2027 per Google at I/O 2026** (2027-01-01 → 2027-12-31), drawn on lanes: poLight (TLens), Q Technology, Cambridge Mechatronics, Camera-module & assembly chain, VCM / SMA actuator makers
- design-in / qualification complete: 2025-07-01 → 2026-12-31
- qualification orders expected: 2025-10-01 → 2027-03-31
- production POs / ramp expected: 2026-07-01 → 2027-09-30
- Rationale: Android XR in-lens display glasses in 2027, stated by Google at I/O 2026; Samsung's colour-display version 'may launch late 2027' and Kering's Gucci glasses 'probably 2027' sit inside the same window. Full-year window, so bands are wide under the 12-18 / 9-15 / 3-6-month norms. Supplier-side signals that fit a 2027-2028 display device are TWedge sample orders from 'a leading AR platform company' (2026-03-07) and from a 'leading consumer OEM' (2026-09-21), but poLight itself says a mass-production-ready TWedge 'could be needed in two years' from Feb 2026, i.e. 2028. Camera autofocus for these glasses is unreported.

**First consumer Specs deliveries, US/UK/France ('later this fall')** (2026-10-01 → 2026-12-31), drawn on lanes: poLight (TLens), Q Technology, Camera-module & assembly chain, Cambridge Mechatronics
- design-in / qualification complete: 2025-04-01 → 2025-12-31
- qualification orders expected: 2025-07-01 → 2026-03-31
- production POs / ramp expected: 2026-04-01 → 2026-09-30
- Rationale: Snap Specs, US$2,195, shipping 'later this fall' 2026 in the US, UK and France; two RGB and two IR cameras with no megapixel or focus-type specification published as of 2026-10-06; initial run reportedly capped at about 100,000 units. Norms as above put design-in in Apr-Dec 2025 and production POs in Apr-Sep 2026. poLight's Program B design-in on 2026-07-10 with Q4 2026 deliveries is 83 days before the window opens, late for a first build unless camera modules are assembled in Q3-Q4; Snap is a U.S. company but poLight labels Program B a 'leading consumer OEM' rather than 'top tier U.S.'. No supplier evidence names Snap.

**Xreal Aura ships fall 2026 in US, UK, Japan, Canada, South Korea; ≤US$1,500** (2026-09-01 → 2026-11-30), drawn on lanes: poLight (TLens), Q Technology, Camera-module & assembly chain
- design-in / qualification complete: 2025-03-01 → 2025-11-30
- qualification orders expected: 2025-06-01 → 2026-02-28
- production POs / ramp expected: 2026-03-01 → 2026-08-30
- Rationale: Xreal Aura, fall 2026 in the US, UK, Japan, Canada and South Korea at no more than US$1,500; two world-facing tracking cameras plus one high-resolution camera with undisclosed focus type. Xreal's 2025 Eye module was explicitly fixed focus, 'autofocus not supported', and Xreal ships only 125-137k units a year, so volumes would be small. Norms as above; Program B's 2026-07-10 design-in is only 53 days before the window start, which argues against Aura unless it slips into Q4. Nothing in the supplier lanes names Xreal; Alibaba and Luxshare are shareholders.

**Qwen AI Glasses N1 / N1 Pro in-stock sale (China)** (2026-10-13 → 2026-10-13), drawn on lanes: Camera-module & assembly chain, VCM / SMA actuator makers, Q Technology
- design-in / qualification complete: 2025-04-13 → 2025-10-13
- qualification orders expected: 2025-07-13 → 2026-01-13
- production POs / ramp expected: 2026-04-13 → 2026-07-13
- Rationale: Alibaba Qwen N1 / N1 Pro glasses with a 50 MP camera, pre-orders 2026-09-22, in-stock sale 2026-10-13, China only. All three phases are already in the past under the norms; a 50 MP sensor implies a larger lens stack where any autofocus would more likely be a VCM-class actuator from the smartphone chain than a 3 mm-aperture tunable lens. The only Alibaba-ecosystem TLens link is Ant Group's 'Look to Pay' demo glasses with a TLens camera on 2026-08-06. No supplier evidence for N1 itself.

**Alibaba next-generation Qwen glasses (unannounced, inferred from 6–10-month cadence)** (2027-01-01 → 2027-12-31), drawn on lanes: Q Technology, Camera-module & assembly chain, poLight (TLens), VCM / SMA actuator makers
- design-in / qualification complete: 2025-07-01 → 2026-12-31
- qualification orders expected: 2025-10-01 → 2027-03-31
- production POs / ramp expected: 2026-07-01 → 2027-09-30
- Rationale: Inferred next-generation Qwen glasses in 2027, based only on Alibaba's 4-6-month release cadence (preview 2025-07-28, launch 2025-11-27, Qwen Glasses 2026-03-03, N1 2026-09-10) with March (MWC) and September (Apsara) as recurring slots. Bands follow the generic norms across a full-year window. Ant Group's TLens demo of 2026-08-06 would sit 5-17 months before a 2027 device, inside the qualification band, but it is a demo, not an order. Evidence is thin and the window itself is unannounced.

**Amazon consumer 'Jayhawk' camera+display glasses (reported late 2026 – early 2027)** (2026-10-01 → 2027-03-31), drawn on lanes: Camera-module & assembly chain, VCM / SMA actuator makers, Q Technology
- design-in / qualification complete: 2025-04-01 → 2026-03-31
- qualification orders expected: 2025-07-01 → 2026-06-30
- production POs / ramp expected: 2026-04-01 → 2026-12-31
- Rationale: Amazon consumer 'Jayhawk' camera-plus-monocular-display glasses, reported by The Information on 2025-09-10 for 'late 2026 or early 2027' with no follow-up reporting in the 391 days since, suggesting slippage. Bands follow the norms. Amazon's AR patents linked to Jayhawk cover UI and fulfilment, not optics or autofocus, and its only shipping glasses are driver units with 'integrated cameras' scaling to 20,000-plus devices by end-2027, a volume too small to show in any supplier's accounts. No supplier signal points at Amazon.

**Pico Space Pro release (Q4 2026, China first; global 'late 2026')** (2026-10-01 → 2026-12-31), drawn on lanes: Q Technology, Camera-module & assembly chain, poLight (TLens), VCM / SMA actuator makers
- design-in / qualification complete: 2025-04-01 → 2025-12-31
- qualification orders expected: 2025-07-01 → 2026-03-31
- production POs / ramp expected: 2026-04-01 → 2026-09-30
- Rationale: Pico Space Pro, Q4 2026 (China first) after the 2026-09-02 debut event was cancelled on 2026-08-25; a social leak claims the first week of October at US$2,899. Q Tech named PICO among the AR brands it covers in its FY2023 deck and lists a 'VR VST TLens AF camera' on its product map, so Pico is the most plausible non-Meta host for that line. Under the norms, production POs should already have been placed (Apr-Sep 2026); poLight's Program B ('leading consumer OEM', design-in 2026-07-10, Q4 deliveries) would fit this window on timing, though poLight describes that customer as 'a major player in the ecosystem'. No teardown yet.

**Viture Helix enterprise AI safety glasses (12MP camera) ship Q1 2027** (2027-01-01 → 2027-03-31), drawn on lanes: poLight (TLens), Q Technology, Camera-module & assembly chain
- design-in / qualification complete: 2025-07-01 → 2026-03-31
- qualification orders expected: 2025-10-01 → 2026-06-30
- production POs / ramp expected: 2026-07-01 → 2026-12-31
- Rationale: Viture Helix enterprise AI safety glasses on NVIDIA XR AI, 12 MP camera, from US$600, shipping Q1 2027; focus type unstated. Bands follow the norms. poLight's enterprise cadence (repeat MR head-mounted design-win with an initial mass-production PO of ~NOK 0.4m on 2025-10-09; IRIS-scanner design-in 2025-10-20) is the type of signal that would fit a Q1 2027 enterprise device, but nothing names Viture, and Viture is being sued by Xreal. Thin.

**Xiaomi AI Glasses 2nd gen (rumoured Q4 2026 – 2027, denied by Xiaomi)** (2026-10-01 → 2027-12-31), drawn on lanes: Camera-module & assembly chain, VCM / SMA actuator makers, Q Technology, poLight (TLens)
- design-in / qualification complete: 2025-04-01 → 2026-12-31
- qualification orders expected: 2025-07-01 → 2027-03-31
- production POs / ramp expected: 2026-04-01 → 2027-09-30
- Rationale: Xiaomi AI Glasses second generation, rumoured on 2026-08-05 to be 'R&D complete' but paused and possibly slipping to Q4 2026 or 2027; Xiaomi called the report false. Generation one (2025-06-26) used a 12 MP Sony IMX681 with Goertek as ODM. Xiaomi was poLight's first consumer TLens customer (Mitu watch, 2020-01-07) and used a NEXTLENS liquid lens in the Mi MIX Fold (2021-03-30), so it has a history of adopting tunable optics, but there is no signal for gen 2. Bands follow the norms over a rumor window.

### 4.2 Matches

**Program A qualification PO #1 (Aug 2025) vs Meta VR Glasses spring 2027** — strength: circumstantial
- Supplier signal: 2025-08-06 · poLight (TLens) · Program A PO #1: ~NOK 1.7m TLens+driver for top-tier consumer OEM qualification (50% firm) (https://www.polight.com/mfn_news/polight-asa-announces-tlens-purchase-order-supporting-a-top-tier-consumer-oem-qualification-program/)
- Producer side: 2027-03-01 → 2027-05-31 · Meta · Meta VR Glasses retail launch — US$1,299.99, 25 countries, autofocus passthrough ("Spring 2027") (https://developers.meta.com/vr/discover/meta-vr-glasses/)
- The first Program A PO (2025-08-06, ~NOK 1.7m, 50% firm) sits 18.8-21.8 months before the Mar-May 2027 window, at the front edge of the 12-18-month design-in norm, which is what a qualification programme that must close by mid-2026 for a spring-2027 build would look like; poLight's Q2-2025 report added that the OEM 'has moved forward with programmes to prepare for the use of TLens'. It would be contradicted by the Q4-2026 report (2027-02-24) still calling Program A 'qualification', by no production PO appearing in the Sep 2026-Feb 2027 band, or by a VR Glasses teardown showing a non-piezo actuator.

**Program A PO #3 (Jun 2026) lands 8.4-11.4 months before spring 2027** — strength: circumstantial
- Supplier signal: 2026-06-18 · poLight (TLens) · Program A PO #3: NOK 0.7m follow-on for ongoing top-tier consumer OEM qualification (https://www.polight.com/mfn_news/polight-asa-announces-follow-on-tlens-purchase-order-supporting-top-tier-consumer-oem-qualification-program-2/)
- Producer side: 2027-03-01 → 2027-05-31 · Meta · Meta VR Glasses retail launch — US$1,299.99, 25 countries, autofocus passthrough ("Spring 2027") (https://developers.meta.com/vr/discover/meta-vr-glasses/)
- PO #3 (NOK 0.7m, 2026-06-18, deliveries 'likely second half of 2026') is 256-347 days before the window; the spacing of the three POs, 189 then 127 days, is accelerating rather than fading, and a final qualification delivery 6-9 months before launch is where the 9-15-month norm puts it. Against this, the CEO still cites 'several milestones to pass', leaves ramp timing 'to be seen', and says the Q Tech line is not sized for the OEM's volume. A Q3-2026 report (2026-10-29) without Program A progress, or Program A deliveries slipping into 2027, would break the fit.

**Q Tech 'Overseas Key Customers: Ramp in 2H26/2027' vs the 3-6-month ramp band** — strength: circumstantial
- Supplier signal: 2026-08-24 · Q Technology · Q Tech H1-2026: "TLens + AR Mono Engine: Volume Delivery Achieved"; "Overseas Key Customers: Ramp in 2H26 / 2027" (https://www1.hkexnews.hk/listedco/listconews/sehk/2026/0824/2026082400333.pdf)
- Producer side: 2027-03-01 → 2027-05-31 · Meta · Meta VR Glasses retail launch — US$1,299.99, 25 countries, autofocus passthrough ("Spring 2027") (https://developers.meta.com/vr/discover/meta-vr-glasses/)
- Published 2026-08-24, 187 days before the window; '2H26/2027' straddles a late-2026 glasses launch (Program B) and a spring-2027 device, and 'TLens + AR Mono Engine: Volume Delivery Achieved' is the first volume wording, 161 days after 'small-batch deliveries' on 2026-03-16. The customer is unnamed and 'overseas' could equally mean Samsung, Google or Snap; Q Tech also now owns TDK's VCM/SMA actuator business, so a ramp need not be TLens. If Q Tech's FY2026 deck (Mar 2027) pushes the ramp to 2H27 or drops TLens from the roadmap, the match fails.

**'US M brand' vendor code (Aug 2024) to the VR Glasses announcement 25 months later** — strength: circumstantial
- Supplier signal: 2024-08-12 · Q Technology · Q Tech H1-2024 deck: "Obtained US M brand Vendor code … target to crack in within this year" (https://www.qtechsmartvision.com/ueditor/php/upload/file/20250226/1740565898224901.pdf)
- Producer side: 2026-09-23 · Meta · Connect 2026: Meta VR Glasses announced, US$1,299.99, spring 2027, passthrough with autofocus (https://www.meta.com/blog/meta-vr-glasses-announcement-meta-connect/)
- Q Tech's H1-2024 deck (2024-08-12) said it had 'obtained US M brand vendor code' with a design-win target 'within this year'; the poLight investment 'backed by a top tier U.S. consumer electronics customer' followed 246 days later and Connect 2026 came 772 days later, with the spring-2027 window 30.6 months out, a plausible new-platform camera cycle. 'M' is never expanded in any deck, and Q Tech also names PICO and HTC as XR customers. The forum claim that Q Tech supplies Meta Quest tracking and passthrough modules has no primary source; if that business is VST modules only with the AF lens sourced elsewhere, the vendor code says nothing about TLens.

**'VR VST TLens AF camera' on Q Tech's product map 18.6 months before the only VR device announced with autofocus passthrough** — strength: circumstantial
- Supplier signal: 2025-08-11 · Q Technology · Q Tech H1-2025 deck lists "VR VST TLens AF camera" and "AR SWIS TLens AF camera"; "2026: TLens" (https://www.qtechsmartvision.com/ueditor/php/upload/file/20250811/1754895156144602.pdf)
- Producer side: 2027-03-01 → 2027-05-31 · Meta · Meta VR Glasses retail launch — US$1,299.99, 25 countries, autofocus passthrough ("Spring 2027") (https://developers.meta.com/vr/discover/meta-vr-glasses/)
- The H1-2025 deck (2025-08-11) lists 'VR VST TLens AF camera' and 'AR SWIS TLens AF camera' and the roadmap entry '2026: TLens'; 408 days later Meta's developer page documents 'passthrough with autofocus and depth', and the 18.6-month lead to 2027-03-01 is at the top of the design-in norm. Pico Space Pro (Q4 2026) and future Galaxy XR units are alternative VST hosts, and Q Tech's 2024 deck named PICO and HTC as customers. A Space Pro teardown showing a TLens would re-attribute the line; a VR Glasses teardown showing another actuator would kill it.

**Ex-Meta engineer: piezo tunable-lens AF module passed AR/VR/MR reliability qualification by Apr 2025** — strength: circumstantial
- Supplier signal: 2025-04 · Meta patents & people · Lidu Huang leaves Meta (Sep 2020–Apr 2025); LinkedIn: piezo tunable-lens AF module 'passing full reliability qualifications for AR/VR/MR' (https://www.linkedin.com/in/lidu-h-3825903)
- Producer side: 2027-03-01 → 2027-05-31 · Meta · Meta VR Glasses retail launch — US$1,299.99, 25 countries, autofocus passthrough ("Spring 2027") (https://developers.meta.com/vr/discover/meta-vr-glasses/)
- Lidu Huang's Meta tenure (Sep 2020-Apr 2025) ends 22 months before the window and 16.8 months before the announcement; a module 'passing full reliability qualifications for AR/VR/MR' by then matches poLight's account that the OEM had qualified TLens 'for many years' and 'triggered' the Q Tech deal. The profile never says whose lens: Meta's own filings describe a window/polymer/glass-membrane/piezo stack that could be built by another MEMS line. If the module was the wristband-camera family Meta abandoned on 2025-05-08, or if no poLight production PO appears by Q1 2027, the inference weakens.

**US 12,663,619 (granted 2026-06-23): wide-FOV tunable-lens assembly citing three poLight documents** — strength: circumstantial
- Supplier signal: 2026-06-23 · Meta patents & people · US 12,663,619 B2 granted to Meta (wide-FOV tunable lens) citing three poLight TLens documents (https://image-ppubs.uspto.gov/dirsearch-public/print/downloadPdf/12663619)
- Producer side: 2027-03-01 → 2027-05-31 · Meta · Meta VR Glasses retail launch — US$1,299.99, 25 countries, autofocus passthrough ("Spring 2027") (https://developers.meta.com/vr/discover/meta-vr-glasses/)
- Meta's side retrieved poLight's TLens Add-In and Packaged TLens pages on 2022-10-19, 1,343 days before this grant, and the example design (107 deg FOV, F/2.2, TTL 5.0 mm, PZT thin film) is a passthrough-class camera; the grant arrives 8 months before the launch window. Citations prove awareness, not purchase, and the lens-barrel grant US 12,631,847 (2026-05-19) could mean Meta integrates a bought TLens or its own membrane lens. The clean contradiction would be a VR Glasses teardown naming a different lens maker, or poLight ending 2027 with no production PO.

**Meta's own paper against autofocus in all-day glasses vs Ray-Ban Meta Gen 3 (fixed focus line)** — strength: strong

- Reality Labs' 2025-04-17 paper says autofocus actuators 'add size and weight... and require more power' and favours fixed focus for always-on glasses; Gen 2 reviews of 2025-12-11 confirmed 'simple fixed focus' and Gen 3 (2026-09-23, 12 MP, 3K, same-day ship) makes no autofocus claim. This is a negative match: it caps TLens's 2027 Meta opportunity at the puck-powered VR Glasses rather than the 7-10m-unit Ray-Ban line. A Gen 3 teardown finding an actuator would overturn it.

## 5. Commentary per lane — what each timeline indicates

### Meta (producer; 69 dated signals)

Meta Platforms / Reality Labs: Quest headsets, Ray-Ban/Oakley/Meta-brand AI glasses with EssilorLuxottica, Meta Ray-Ban Display and the Orion AR prototype. Every camera glasses shipped to date is fixed-focus; the spring-2027 Meta VR Glasses (ex-Phoenix) are the first Meta consumer device whose passthrough cameras are documented as autofocus, making Meta the single most important potential top-tier customer for a tunable-lens or VCM autofocus supplier. Meta Connect 2026 (2026-09-23/24) announced Meta VR Glasses for a spring-2027 launch, the central product for the poLight question; see the Meta file. Included here only as calendar intersections. Meta appears only indirectly: poLight's Q3 2025 deck credits Ray-Ban Meta (>2M units in 2024) as the consumer driver, and the user's brief places a Meta autofocus-camera 'VR Glasses' launch in spring 2027. No poLight document names Meta. Meta as producer: display-side Butterscotch varifocal prototype (2023) and the 2026-09-23 announcement of VR glasses for spring 2027, which falls days after Meta's lens-barrel patent publication and Optotune's glasses-class lens launch. Meta's glasses lineup (Ray-Ban Meta Gen 1/2, Oakley HSTN/Vanguard, Ray-Ban Display, Meta Glasses) all carry 12 MP fixed-focus cameras per teardowns; capacity constraints and doubling plans in early 2026. The Meta VR Glasses announced for spring 2027 are the product window that Q Tech's 2H26/2027 ramp and DBS's 1H27 claim would fit. Meta as a producer: the research brief posits 'VR glasses' with autofocus cameras in spring 2027. The 2022-07 / 2023-02 patent priority dates imply a 3.5–4.5-year priority-to-product lag, consistent with reliability qualification during 2024 and production readiness by April 2025. Product verification is outside this file's scope. Meta announced its ~100 g Meta VR Glasses at Connect 2026 (23 Sept 2026) for spring 2027 at USD 1,299.99, with developer specs confirming color stereoscopic 26-PPD passthrough 'with autofocus and depth sensing' but naming no actuator or supplier. Also the dominant AI-glasses OEM (Ray-Ban Display, ~94% share H1 2026).

Cadence: 49 day/month-dated non-press signals; median gap 14 days overall, 13 days since 2025; longest silence 364 days.

**2019-09-17 → 2023-10-17: Fixed-focus glasses, camera headsets**  
Luxottica co-development began 2019-09-17; Quest 2 shipped 2020-10-13 without colour passthrough; Ray-Ban Stories (2021-09-09) were torn down as fixed focus with 'no voice coil or magnets'. Quest Pro (2022-10-25) and Quest 3 (teased 2023-06-01, shipped 2023-10-10, 131 days, 2x4 MP passthrough) added colour passthrough without autofocus, and Ray-Ban Meta shipped 20 days after its 2023-09-27 announcement. The pattern: glasses ship within weeks of announcement, headsets within months.

**2024-08-29 → 2026-02-25: Roadmap churn**  
'Puffin' was first reported on 2024-08-29 for 2027, pulled in to end-2026 (2025-06-02), delayed to H1 2027 (2025-12-06 memos) and confirmed by Bosworth on 2026-02-10 alongside Quest 4 'Griffin' (no earlier than H2 2027). Orion was shown 2024-09-25 with commercial AR 'expected 2027'; the EssilorLuxottica long-term deal (2024-09-17) and ~3% stake (2025-07-09) underpinned 2m Ray-Ban Meta by Feb 2025 and 7m AI glasses in 2025. Meta Ray-Ban Display shipped 13 days after its 2025-09-17 reveal (~84k units in 2025) and Gen 2 was confirmed 'simple fixed focus' on 2025-12-11.

**2026-06-23 → 2027-05-31: Connect 2026 and the autofocus line**  
The own-brand Meta Glasses line launched 2026-06-23; Reality Labs lost US$4.62bn on US$431m revenue in Q2. Connect 2026 (2026-09-23) announced Meta VR Glasses at US$1,299.99 for spring 2027 with 'passthrough with autofocus and depth' (159-250 days announce-to-ship versus 13-34 days for glasses), shipped Ray-Ban Meta Gen 3 the same day, and scheduled the camera-free Audio (2026-10-13) and Adventurer (2026-10-23). Bosworth said on 2026-09-24 there is 'no new version' of Display hardware and would not commit to AR glasses in 2027. The spring-2027 window therefore carries Meta's first and only documented autofocus camera.

### Apple (producer; 31 dated signals)

Apple ships only Vision Pro (2024-02-02; M5 refresh 2025-10-22) and has no glasses yet. Gurman/Kuo put the display-less N50 glasses (two cameras: capture + computer vision) at a WWDC June 2027 unveil, Q2 2027 mass production and late-2027 shipment, after slipping from end-2026. Apple is the most-cited alternative to Meta as poLight's "Top Tier U.S. Consumer Electronics OEM", but no camera-module or focus-actuator vendor is public and the camera configuration was still undecided in July 2026. Apple WWDC 2026 (week of June 8) is listed only as a calendar intersection; see the Apple file. No supplier or autofocus detail reported. Apple's tunable-lens filings (2018, 2023, 2024; inventors including ex-CML CTO Richard Topliss) are for fluid-filled see-through prescription lenses in head-mounted devices, not camera autofocus. No Apple glasses-camera AF tunable-lens filing surfaced; Apple camera architect Patrick Llull moved to Meta in May 2026. Strongest alternative identity for poLight's unnamed top-tier U.S. OEM if not Meta; Vision Pro sold ~390k in 2024 and first lighter smart glasses are reported for late 2027. Q Tech's lack of Apple exposure argues against Apple being the Q Tech-backed customer.

### poLight (TLens) (supplier; 132 dated signals)

poLight's qualification customer is a "Top Tier U.S. Consumer Electronics OEM"; the 2025-10-13 PO was explicitly for the design of a TLens camera for AR applications, and a follow-on order puts deliveries likely in H2 2026. Among Apple, Google and Samsung, only Apple and Google are U.S. companies, and neither has any public autofocus or actuator disclosure for their glasses; the H2 2026 delivery window lines up better with Meta than with Apple's Q2-2027 mass production. poLight's disclosed AR design wins are Vuzix Shield (industrial, dual TLens autofocus cameras, April 2024) and SnkeXR (medical, Oct 2025), plus an unnamed consumer-electronics AR/MR device with a NOK 2.4m follow-on order (April 2026) and a 'first consumer AR/MR design-in' disclosed in August 2026 for a 2026 launch. None of the six OEMs in this file has a public link to poLight. Only historical design-in evidence in this scope: Yole records Xiaomi's Mitu 4Pro (Jan 2020) as the first commercial TLens product. No link from poLight to any of the glasses OEMs covered here was found. poLight ASA (Oslo Børs: PLT) sells the TLens piezo-MEMS tunable lens, already inside enterprise AR/MR cameras (Magic Leap 2, Vuzix Shield, SnkeXR). Three separate consumer AR|MR programs are public: Program A (Q Tech-backed top-tier U.S. OEM qualification, POs Aug 2025/Feb 2026/Jun 2026), Program B (leading consumer OEM, reclassified as first consumer design-in on 2026-07-10 with launch expected 2026) and Program C (NOK 5m AR camera design for a top-tier U.S. OEM). poLight never names Meta and has never stated 2027. poLight reference points found in this file: invited SPIE AR/VR/MR talks in 2025 and 2026 (where CML was absent), the Q Tech partnership backed by a 'US top-tier customer' and TLens's '<6 mW' power claim used as the benchmark against CML SMA. poLight (fabless, Norway) makes the TLens PZT-piezo tunable lens; ST fabs the actuator wafers and Asian partners assemble. AR2025 reports no new MEMS wafers ordered in 2025, 55% of deliveries to AR/MR, and three POs tied to a Top-Tier U.S. consumer OEM AR program; a fresh ST wafer order would be a leading indicator of a volume ramp. poLight ASA (Oslo), maker of the TLens piezo-MEMS autofocus lens fabbed at ST's Agrate fab. Its releases track qualification orders from the Q Tech-linked top-tier US OEM (Aug 2025, Oct 2025, Feb 2026, Jun 2026), a separate first consumer AR/MR design-in (Aug 2026), and the progress of the Q Tech TLens line; the only teardown-confirmed XR win is Magic Leap 2. poLight's own IP (2006 → 2025, including the Phair and Tallaron patents cited by Meta), its SPIE invited talks in Jan 2025 and Jan 2026, and the Oct 2025 – Sep 2026 order stream from an unnamed top-tier US consumer OEM for TLens cameras in AR head-worn devices, including a qualification-program PO and a mass-price strategic TLens PO. Recent filings (lead-free TLens, low-power driver) point at consumer-qualification requirements. Norwegian supplier of the TLens piezo-MEMS tunable-lens autofocus (TLens Silver, Add-In, PD50 driver) and TWedge. Runs three separate consumer programs: the Q Tech-backed top-tier U.S. OEM qualification (A), the Oct-2025 ~NOK 5m AR head-worn camera project (B) and the 2026 consumer AR/MR design-in (C). poLight has never named Meta.

Cadence: 115 day/month-dated non-press signals; median gap 8 days overall, 5 days since 2025; longest silence 1668 days.

**2005-01-01 → 2019-12-31: IP and foundry foundations**  
Founded in Horten in 2005, with the core TLens patent priorities from 2006-09-21 (expiring from September 2026) and the Meta-cited filings of 2014-07-18 and 2017-06-30. ST became the thin-film piezo partner in 2014-09 with 'volume production H2 2015', after an SVTC deal in 2011 that already targeted ~1M units/month; the 2017 plan was 400 to 1,000 wafers/month (0.9 to 2.25M lenses/month). The Oslo listing came on 2018-10-01. Capacity ambitions ran a decade ahead of any consumer volume.

**2020-01-01 → 2024-12-31: First wins, slow XR**  
The Xiaomi Mitu watch shipped the first consumer TLens in January 2020, a barcode design-win followed on 2020-09-18 and 2021 decks cited ~19 AR projects with three design-wins expected in 1H 2022. The Magic Leap 2 teardown (2023-03-02) and Vuzix Shield (2024-04-08) were the only XR wins made public, both enterprise. FY2024 revenue was NOK 9.6m with cash of NOK 166.8m and TLens in 28 products; the first TWedge sample PO (~NOK 500k, 2024-11-21) from a 'top-tier consumer OEM' opened the current chapter.

**2025-04-15 → 2026-06-18: Q Tech alliance and Program A**  
Q Tech bought 32.97% at NOK 2.69 (NOK 171.5m) on 2025-04-15, a deal management said one long-standing top-tier U.S. OEM 'triggered'. Program A qualification POs followed on 2025-08-06 (~NOK 1.7m, 50% firm), 2026-02-11 (final 50%, 189 days later) and 2026-06-18 (NOK 0.7m, 127 days later) with deliveries 'likely second half of 2026' and 'still several milestones to pass'. Cash rose from NOK 135.8m (Q1 2025) to NOK 298.2m (Q3 2025) and the share price from 6.21 to 9.75 across the two 2026 announcements; no new MEMS wafers were ordered in 2025 because inventory covers several million lenses.

**2025-11-24 → 2026-07-10: Program B from PoC to design-in**  
A 'leading consumer OEM' placed POs of NOK 0.9m (2025-11-24, 'mature proof-of-concept'), 2.4m (2026-04-07, 134 days later), 0.8m (2026-05-20, 43 days) and 0.8m (2026-07-10, 51 days), NOK 4.9m in 228 days, and on the fourth PO poLight declared its first consumer AR|MR design-in with the product 'expected to launch in 2026' and deliveries in Q4 2026. Wording moved from 'promising sign' to 'not without its challenges and risks' to 'mass production POs'. AR|MR reached 86% of Q2 2026 revenue and H1 2026 revenue (NOK 20.6m) exceeded all of FY2025 (NOK 20.5m); the pipeline shows 6 design-wins and 0 design-ins in AR/MR as of Q1 2026, before this relabel.

**2026-08-06 → 2027-02-24: Watch window**  
On the Q2 call (2026-08-06) the CEO said the design-in customer is separate from the Q Tech-backed OEM ('but there are more'); the earlier ChatGPT thread also read a post-quarter Q&A as separating the third programme, a document the fact-check could not locate, priced consumer TLens at ~US$2+, and said assembly and test capacity would double by end-2026. Insiders sold 1,109,586 shares at NOK 12.13 on 2026-08-27; the stock closed at NOK 14.90 on 2026-09-25 (~NOK 3.2bn market cap). Program C modules shipped for validation at end-Q2 2026 and 'more visibility' on Programs A and C is promised towards year-end. The Q3 report (2026-10-29), the Capital Markets Day (2026-10-30) and the Q4 report (2027-02-24, five days before the VR Glasses window) are the dated checkpoints; the 3-6-month production-PO band for a spring-2027 device runs Sep 2026-Feb 2027 and nothing has been announced in it as of 2026-10-06.

### Q Technology (supplier; 23 dated signals)

Q Technology, poLight's strategic investor and module partner. The only Meta link in this file is a Xueqiu forum post asserting Q Tech supplies Quest tracking/passthrough modules and that its TLens tie-up positions it for Meta glasses; no HKEX/IR primary source exists. Q Technology (HK:1478) bought 32.97% of poLight in April 2025 'with backing from a top tier U.S. consumer electronics customer' and built a dedicated TLens assembly/test line (equipment installed Oct 2025, lenses qualified by Aug 2026, NRE invoiced USD 313k + 179k). Its FY2025 deck places TLens and TWedge on a 2026 XR roadmap, making it the module-level bridge between poLight and the unnamed OEM. Q Technology is simultaneously poLight's largest shareholder and TLens production partner, and (via its controlling shareholder) the new owner of TDK's camera-module actuator business with 2,000+ patents and SMA routes. Its FY2025 deck puts TLens on the 2026 roadmap and targets Top-2 actuator rank, so a Q Tech-built XR camera module could use either technology. Q Technology invested NOK 171.5 M in poLight on 2025-04-15 'backed by a top tier U.S. consumer electronics customer' and is building a dedicated TLens assembly/test line in China; poLight invoiced USD 313,141 of NRE in March 2026 for qualifying it for mass production, with work continuing through 2026. Q Technology (1478.HK), Kunshan camera-module maker and poLight's largest shareholder (~30%), building a dedicated TLens assembly-and-test line and, via its controlling shareholder, absorbing TDK's actuator unit (Actutek). Its semi-annual decks give the only dated primary-source ladder from "US M brand vendor code" (Aug 2024) to "TLens volume delivery achieved" and "Overseas Key Customers ramp 2H26/2027" (Aug 2026), but no filing names Meta. Q Technology, poLight's manufacturing partner: Chris Liu (GM Q Tech IoT BU since Oct 2021) and Louis So sit on poLight's board, and the Q3-2025 deck reports a TLens assembly and final-test line at Q Tech under process qualification, with initial lens yield 'not so bad' by Q1 2026. Relevant as the capacity leg of any top-tier OEM design-in. Camera-module maker and poLight's largest shareholder (~NOK 171m, ~one-third stake since June 2025), backed by a top-tier U.S. customer; building a dedicated TLens assembly/test line. Holds a 'US M brand' XR vendor code since 2024 and absorbed TDK's actuator business, so it could supply Meta with VCM or TLens modules.

Cadence: 20 day/month-dated non-press signals; median gap 31 days overall, 29 days since 2025; longest silence 892 days.

**2021-10-01 → 2025-04-28: Vendor code to alliance**  
Chris Liu took over the IoT business unit in October 2021. The FY2023 deck (2024-03-25) reported a 'vendor code of major US customer'; 140 days later the H1-2024 deck named it 'US M brand' with a design-win target 'within this year'. The FY2024 deck (2025-03-18) said Q Tech was 'qualified as the supplier for multiple international leading manufacturers' of VR/AR/AI glasses; 28 days later it invested NOK 171.5m in poLight 'backed by a top tier U.S. consumer electronics customer', and 13 days after that signed the framework to buy TDK's camera-actuator business, including SMA routes and a Philippines plant.

**2025-06-09 → 2026-03-31: Line build and roadmap**  
The HKEX note of 2025-06-09 called poLight's piezo actuator 'the best solution for XR optical modules'. The H1-2025 deck (2025-08-11) put 'VR VST TLens AF camera' and 'AR SWIS TLens AF camera' on the product map with a '2026: Light Engine, TLens, TWedge, AR DSA' roadmap. By Q3 2025 the dedicated TLens line had 'all equipment installed' with capacity comparable to the Philippines line; poLight invoiced USD 313,141 of 2025 NRE in March 2026 for 'getting it qualified for mass production'. The FY2025 deck (2026-03-16) reported 'small-batch deliveries for 2 leading overseas terminal clients', 10 brands, and the TDK closing 'well underway' (completed around 2026-03-31).

**2026-04-24 → 2026-10-05: Volume wording**  
The annual report (2026-04-24) showed a 29.96% poLight stake and a RMB 15.1m share of loss. The H1-2026 deck (2026-08-24) moved to 'TLens + AR Mono Engine: Volume Delivery Achieved', 'Deliveries to 2 Top Overseas OEMs', '22 Partners (+4)' and 'Overseas Key Customers: Ramp in 2H26 / 2027', 161 days after 'small-batch'; group revenue was RMB 9.92bn (+12.4%) with R&D up 24.2% to RMB 346.7m focused on AI/AR glasses. DBS initiated BUY (TP HKD 12.60) on 2026-08-26 citing 'low-power tunable lenses'. A Xueqiu post tying Q Tech's modules to Meta Quest (2026-09-22) has no primary source; the IR head bought 218,000 shares with a one-year lock on 2026-10-05.

### Cambridge Mechatronics (supplier; 58 dated signals)

Cambridge Mechatronics (UK) designs SMA wire actuators, controller ICs and firmware, licensed to Alps Alpine, GalaxyCore, TDK and others, shipping in 100M+ smartphones. It is the most credible mechanical alternative to poLight's TLens for autofocus in glasses, with a five-year trail of anonymous 'commercial engagements' with US tech giants in AR/VR, a 2026 deformable-lens patent for glasses/headsets and a job ad citing 'AI glasses' — but no named XR customer, order or production disclosure. Cambridge Mechatronics: SMA actuator maker with 'commercial engagements with US technology giants' since Oct 2021, SPIE XR talks in 2022–2023, a $40m raise in 2024, and 2024-priority filings (WO2025186538/9, WO2026087900) for SMA-deformed liquid/gel lenses in AR/VR headsets. Meta's SMA-flavoured two-stage-actuator application citing CML was allowed then abandoned in May 2025. Ex-CML CTOs Topliss and Brown now appear at Apple. Cambridge Mechatronics, the strongest competitor candidate: SMA AF+OIS actuators in mass production via licensees (Alps Alpine), Zero Hold Power, and a 2026 deformable-liquid-lens application. Its patents appear in Meta filings and it cites 'US technology giant' engagements, but its licensing model leaves no public order trail; SMA versus piezo is the key technical distinction.

Cadence: 56 day/month-dated non-press signals; median gap 32 days overall, 32 days since 2025; longest silence 6985 days.

**1995-06-21 → 2021-08-06: Smartphone-scale SMA**  
Incorporated 1995-06-21; first SMA OIS design wins with Hutchinson and Foxconn shipped in September 2014; a TDK manufacturing licence followed in 2017 (TDK had bought Hutchinson for US$126m in 2015). 2021 brought ultra-slim OIS+AF (2021-04-21), first driver ICs (2021-07-02) and a Tokyo office. Everything shipped was smartphone volume, tens of millions of units.

**2021-10-18 → 2024-02-13: XR push**  
The San Francisco office opening (2021-10-18) claimed 'multiple commercial engagements with the US technology giants... AR/VR devices'; SPIE AR/VR/MR talks followed on 2022-01-20, 2023-01-25 ('already engaged in commercial projects with several industry leaders') and 2024-01-29, with a CES 2023 showcase of zero-hold-power focus for displays and cameras. Patents climbed from 500 (2021-12-22) to 700 (2024-02-09) and the US$40m round closed 2024-02-13 with Intel Capital and Sony. Meta's 2022-05-31 wearable-camera filing cited four CML documents, the only producer trace of these engagements.

**2024-03-04 → 2026-10-06: Pivot and silence**  
Two XR patent priorities (2024-03-04 and 2024-10-24) describe SMA-driven deformable or liquid lenses 'for glasses and... VR/AR headsets' (published 2025-09-12 and 2026-04-30), but CML was absent from SPIE 2025 and 2026 and its last XR-facing article is dated 2025-05-12, 512 days ago. A new CEO arrived 2025-04-10, Meta abandoned its CML-citing application on 2025-05-08, and CTO Ben Brown's tenure ended in March 2026 with his headline now 'Apple'. Share allotments on 2024-06-26, 2024-09-26, 2026-04-08 and 2026-06-01 suggest rolling equity raises; the live 'AI Vision Product Manager' ad cites 'AI glasses' and ~850 patents, so the ambition persists without a named XR customer.

### Optotune (supplier; 23 dated signals)

Optotune's EL-3.1-10.8 electromagnetically-actuated liquid lens appears only as a reference row from the brief (20 mW @10 dpt, 1.72 mm thick, ms response, datasheet listing AR/VR); not re-verified here. Optotune (Dietikon, CH) makes voice-coil-actuated shape-changing liquid lenses and is the only tunable-lens rival with a glasses-class part: EL-3.1-10.8 (1.72 mm, 0.3 g, 3.1 mm CA, 20 mW @10 dpt), launched 2026-09-21 but documented for OEMs since 2025-09-17. No XR design win, financing or XR talk found; its AR page promises custom lenses to volume production. Optotune's liquid-lens filings target smart-glasses and AR/VR optics (ring-actuated lens 2023, gravity-coma compensation 2024). Its US20160202455A1 was cited by the EP search report against Meta's lens-barrel family. No Optotune camera-AF presence at SPIE AR/VR/MR 2025–2026. Swiss tunable-lens maker whose EL-3.1-10.8 electromagnetic liquid lens (1.7 mm, 0.3 g, 20-85 mW) launched Sept 2026 targeting AR/VR OEM integration. The clearest identifiable direct technological alternative to TLens, though with higher power consumption.

Cadence: 13 day/month-dated non-press signals; median gap 113 days overall, 90 days since 2025; longest silence 1265 days.

**2008-01-01 → 2023-03-14: Industrial liquid-lens leader**  
ETH spin-off founded 2008; Slovakia plant 2017; 100 employees 2018; one-millionth component 2021. The December 2022 brochure listed 250 employees, >300k units/yr cleanroom capacity and AR/VR as a market, while the EL-3-10 datasheet (revisions 2020-12-01 and 2023-03-14) remained a 3 mm industrial part. Stanford's 2017 focus-tunable VR display work used Optotune lenses, the lane's only XR research trace.

**2023-08-30 → 2026-09-21: A glasses-class part surfaces**  
Eyewear-specific patents were filed 2023-08-30 (non-circular ring-actuated lens) and 2024-06-24 (gravity-coma compensation for smart glasses). The EL-3.1-10.8 handling instructions carry a 2025-09-17 date, a US Sales Manager for 'industrial and consumer applications' was posted 2026-01-08, and the datasheet (2026-09-18: 1.72 mm high, 0.3 g, 3.1 mm aperture, 20 mW at 10 dpt, 1 ms response) preceded the LinkedIn launch on 2026-09-21, 369 days after the handling document and two days before Connect 2026. Headcount is now 180+ (from 250) with 2+ million units shipped; no customer is named and the CCM page lists phones, action cams and robotics.

### Corning Varioptic (supplier; 10 dated signals)

Corning Varioptic (Lyon) sells electrowetting liquid lenses positioned explicitly 'for industrial applications'; packaged parts are ≥3.5 mm thick. Corning's AR role is high-index waveguide glass, not autofocus, and its 2026-27 event calendar is entirely machine vision. Corning Varioptic electrowetting liquid lenses offer barrel-free autofocus but are positioned for industrial, scientific and medical vision rather than consumer AR; no public Meta link was found. poLight names Corning among its tunable-optics competitors.

Cadence: 6 day/month-dated non-press signals; median gap 958 days overall, 1010 days since 2025; longest silence 2166 days.

**2007-01-01 → 2019-06-30: Pioneer, then Corning**  
Liquid autofocus lenses deployed since 2007 and a smartphone OIS+AF lens (B617, 2011-01-24, 'up to 90% less power' than VCM) never reached phone volume; Parrot sold Varioptic to Invenios (2016-12-29) and Corning absorbed it in 2017, nearly doubling capacity in 2018 and delivering the 2-millionth lens by June 2019, all to machine vision, barcode and medical.

**2022-01-28 → 2027-05-31: Industrial only**  
Corning's XR presence is high-index waveguide glass (2022-01-28, 2023-12-19) with no liquid-lens mention; the Varioptic page of 2026-09-24 reads 'adjustable lens solutions for industrial applications' and the Oct 2026-May 2027 show calendar (VISION Stuttgart, ITE, Photonics West, Vision China, Automate) contains no consumer or XR event. Meta's lens-barrel examiner cited Varioptic as prior art only. Seven-plus years without a consumer signal.

### Sheba Microsystems (supplier; 9 dated signals)

Sheba Microsystems (Toronto) offers MEMS sensor-shift autofocus (<10 mW, <5 ms) and launched an AR/VR/XR headset camera on 2024-01-09, but had 17 staff and USD 400K sales in 2026 with no disclosed customers. A June-2026 COO hire signals an industrialization push. Sheba Microsystems offers MEMS sensor-shift autofocus for compact AR/VR cameras; its June 2026 Teledyne MEMS partnership concerns ADAS cameras, not glasses. Judged low-probability for the Meta socket given limited consumer-volume maturity.

Cadence: 8 day/month-dated non-press signals; median gap 121 days overall, 154 days since 2025; longest silence 453 days.

**2015-01-01 → 2024-10-10: Launches without customers**  
Founded 2015 (or 2016), Sheba launched an athermalising MEMS AF actuator on 2023-09-21, a sensor-shift AF camera 'for AR/VR/XR headsets' (<10 mW, <5 ms) on 2024-01-09, and the Sharp-7 automotive AF camera on 2024-05-16 with a DXOMARK report on 2024-10-10; no XR customer has been named in any release.

**2026-01-06 → 2026-06-25: Scale check**  
The CES 2026 Ontario delegation listed 17 staff and USD 400K annual sales (2026-01-06); a COO was appointed 2026-06-09 and Teledyne named as MEMS production partner for ADAS actuators on 2026-06-25. 1,001 days after the XR camera launch the company remains an automotive-leaning start-up, not a candidate for a 2027 top-tier programme.

### VCM / SMA actuator makers (supplier; 14 dated signals)

Samsung Electro-Mechanics (SEMCO) designs and makes camera lenses and actuators in-house and in December 2025 was reported close to its first FP-coil (AF/OIS actuator component) supply to an unnamed "global big tech" whose profile matches Apple, with "potential expansion to XR devices". This is the only actuator-supply signal found around the three OEMs and points to conventional VCM, not tunable lenses. VCM is poLight's named primary competitor ('very tough competition from VCM'). Q Tech's controlling shareholder is acquiring TDK's micro-actuator (VCM) assets, so Q Tech can offer both VCM and TLens camera modules to the same OEM. Conventional VCM/SMA actuator makers: Alps Alpine (CML-licensed SMA mass production since Jan 2023, piezo zoom), MinebeaMitsumi (AF VCM since 2005), TDK's camera-actuator unit (carved out as Actutek, incl. SMA 'memory metal' IP, sold to Q Tech's controlling shareholder) and Korean OIS suppliers. No public evidence of any supplying autofocus actuators for XR glasses. VCM/SMA actuator makers; here the TDK camera-module actuator business (Micro Actuator Solutions) carved into Actutek and acquired by Q Tech's controlling shareholder between April 2025 and June 2026. Shows the Q Tech group now offers VCM/OIS alternatives alongside TLens, so a Q Tech module does not by itself imply TLens. Incumbent conventional actuator makers (MinebeaMitsumi VCM since 2005, Alps Alpine VCM and CML-licensed SMA, TDK's actuator business now in the Q Tech group). Meta patents also describe VCM solutions, so a conventional actuator remains a ~10% alternative in the thread's estimates.

Cadence: 11 day/month-dated non-press signals; median gap 78 days overall, 77 days since 2025; longest silence 2548 days.

**2005-01-01 → 2023-01-24: Incumbents**  
MinebeaMitsumi has built VCM/OIS actuators in Cebu since 2005 and Alps Alpine's bi-directional AF actuator entered mass production 2016-01-19; TDK bought Hutchinson (CML's SMA partner) in 2015-11. Alps started SMA actuator mass production on 2023-01-10 (30 um wire, ~40% smaller than VCM) and sampled a piezo zoom actuator on 2023-01-24, showing the incumbents already own alternatives to piezo membranes.

**2025-04-28 → 2026-09-26: Consolidation into Q Tech's orbit**  
TDK's framework to sell its camera-actuator business to Q Tech's controlling shareholder (2025-04-28) led to the Actutek carve-out (2025-11-14, 200 days later), effectiveness on 2026-01-01 and completed transfer by 2026-06-01 (399 days); Everbright counted 2,000+ patents and 2,000+ employees. LG Innotek held >50% of iPhone 17 folded-zoom OIS (2025-10-01) and SEMCO neared a fine-pitch-coil supply to a 'global big tech' with 'potential expansion to XR devices' (2025-12-17). Alps showed no XR actuator at CES 2026; Mitsumi appears among Meta's VCM patent references (2026-09-26). The coil chain remains the default for any Apple or Samsung camera.

### Camera-module & assembly chain (supplier; 27 dated signals)

Camera-module and assembly chain around Meta: Goertek (Quest, Ray-Ban Meta, Display engine, reportedly Phoenix), Luxshare (Vietnam VR line), Sunny Optical (camera modules), EssilorLuxottica assembly. Analyst and press supply-chain maps place the fixed-focus AI-glasses cameras with Sunny; no source yet names the autofocus camera module or actuator vendor for the 2027 VR Glasses. Camera-module and assembly chain evidence for these OEMs: LG Innotek was reported as the exclusive 3D-sensor supplier for Apple Vision Pro (KED Global, 2024-02-14) while supplying ~70% of iPhone camera modules. Luxshare reportedly stopped Vision Pro production at the start of 2025 (IDC via FT). Camera-module and assembly chain. Within this file only Luxshare Precision (Xreal shareholder) and Goertek (XR reference designs at CES 2026) surface, with no documented module supply relationship to any of the six OEMs; Q Tech, CML, Optotune and Sunny have no public link either. Camera-module makers sit between poLight and the OEM ('it is the OEM… who is kind of the big boss'). SHARP mentioned poLight at a Tech-Day and is in dialogue with poLight; Tongxing hosts poLight's final-test capacity. Sunny Optical claims the global No.1 share in smart-glasses imaging modules (1H25 XR revenue RMB 1.2bn) and showed its own AI glasses at CES 2026. It is a module-level competitor/customer for any actuator supplier, with no stated actuator technology. poLight's manufacturing chain: STMicroelectronics has made the PZT thin-film actuator wafers at its 200 mm Agrate fab since the 2014 TFP launch, and Tong Hsing (THEIL) assembled TLenses from at least 2017 (400→1,000 wafers/month plan). ST is also Metalenz's metasurface foundry; no ST-Meta tunable-lens link was found. Camera-module and assembly chain around glasses: Goertek (Meta Quest/Ray-Ban/Ray-Ban Display assembler), Luxshare (Vision Pro assembly, possible Meta glasses share), Sunny Optical (smart-glasses ramp, AF cameras for AR), Cowell/Largan (Vision Pro modules) and STMicro (poLight's piezo-MEMS foundry). No teardown identifies a camera-module maker or AF actuator in any Meta glasses.

Cadence: 14 day/month-dated non-press signals; median gap 125 days overall, 64 days since 2025; longest silence 2457 days.

**2014-09-23 → 2017-06-30: ST and Tong Hsing backbone**  
ST unveiled its thin-film piezo process on 2014-09-23 with poLight as 'one of the first customers' and Agrate 200 mm volume for mid-2015; by Q1 2017 ST made the actuator and Tong Hsing assembled, with a 400 to 1,000 wafers/month plan (0.9 to 2.25M units/month) and wafers buffered for 1H 2018 delivery. That buffered inventory is why no new wafers were needed in 2025-2026.

**2021-06-01 → 2025-10-30: Meta and Apple assembly chains**  
ST-Metalenz metasurface optics went from partnership (2021-06) to a 300 mm fab licence with >140M units shipped (2025-07-10). Luxshare was the sole Vision Pro contract manufacturer (2023-07-03) and LG Innotek its exclusive 3D-sensor supplier (2024-02-14); Sunny Optical and Goertek were named Quest 3 beneficiaries (2023-09-29), Goertek committed US$280m to Vietnam (2024) and built Hypernova (2025-09-16), with the Display's camera drawing 1.0-1.7 W (2025-10-30). Sunny's 1H25 XR revenue was RMB 1,201.2m (+21.1%) as 'global NO.1' in smart-glasses imaging modules, actuator technology unstated.

**2026-01-05 → 2027-12-31: 2026 ramp signals**  
Luxshare announced a US$520m, 9m-unit/yr Vietnam VR line tied to Meta (2026-01-05); ChinaTalk mapped Goertek assembly and Sunny camera modules for Ray-Ban Meta/Display with the Chinese BOM share cut from ~61% to 33.5% (2026-01-07); Counterpoint saw Goertek's share nearly doubling (2026-05-19). Sunny's H1-2026 results (2026-08-26) said smart glasses 'have entered the volume ramp-up phase' and introduced a 'Compact High-Res AF for AR'; DBS (2026-08-28) made the 'FY27 ramp of major smart-glasses programmes' its catalyst; the 2026-09-20 Phoenix preview named Goertek assembly, Sony sensors and Sunny camera modules. The actuator inside those modules is still unnamed anywhere.

### Meta patents & people (supplier; 38 dated signals)

Meta-side patents, papers, job posts and people evidence relevant to autofocus. Includes the 2025 Reality Labs paper arguing against autofocus in all-day AI glasses (a counter-signal), a job post for passthrough camera IQ, piezo tunable-lens AF patents reportedly citing poLight, and a LinkedIn claim of a piezo tunable-lens AF module qualified for AR/VR/MR. Meta patent filings on wearable cameras with low-power autofocus (US20230280637A1, abandoned May 2025; US12493197B2, granted Dec 2025) cite CML and poLight prior art. These are examiner citations, not supply evidence, but show Meta engineering actuator-level AF for wearables. Meta holds a cluster of wearable-camera patents describing a PZT thin-film piezo tunable lens for >100° FOV cameras and a tunable-lens barrel for AR glasses. US 12,663,619 B2 (granted 2026-06-23) cites three poLight TLens documents retrieved 2022-10-19, and ex-Meta inventor Lidu Huang says he led a piezo tunable-lens AF module through AR/VR/MR reliability qualification; no Meta fab or Meta-ST deal was found. Meta Platforms Technologies' patent and people trail: a 2022–2023 cluster of piezo-membrane tunable-lens camera filings (window / polymer / glass membrane / thin-film PZT) granted Dec 2025 – Jun 2026, with US 12,663,619's IDS listing three poLight TLens documents retrieved Oct 2022. Inventor Lidu Huang's LinkedIn says the piezo AF module passed full AR/VR/MR reliability qualification before he left in Apr 2025. This is the strongest circumstantial evidence that Meta's autofocus glasses camera is TLens-adjacent. Meta's patent and people evidence: the granted wide-FOV PZT tunable-autofocus patent US 12,663,619 (citing three 2022 poLight documents), lens-barrel tunable-lens patents and the Sept 2026 continuation, plus filings citing CML and poLight, and a former engineer's LinkedIn claim of a qualified piezoelectric tunable-lens AF module. Shows R&D direction, not the production BOM.

Cadence: 36 day/month-dated non-press signals; median gap 35 days overall, 35 days since 2025; longest silence 476 days.

**2020-09-01 → 2023-08-28: Lidu Huang era filings**  
Across Lidu Huang's tenure (Sep 2020-Apr 2025, 55.9 months) Meta filed a dense tunable-lens stack: wristband AF citing poLight (2022-01-04), two-stage wearable camera citing four CML documents (2022-05-31), macro-mode low-power AF (2022-07-12), 'flat-surfaced tunable optical lens' with PZT film for smart-glasses cameras (2022-07-22), the lens-barrel provisional (2022-07-26), retrieval of poLight's TLens Add-In pages (2022-10-19), an FPCB-mounted tunable lens (2022-11-08), the wide-FOV 107-degree/F2.2/TTL 5.0 mm assembly (2023-02-08) and closed-loop capacitance sensing (2023-08-28, the latest piezo priority). Seven piezo-relevant priorities in 20 months, with the window/polymer/glass-membrane/piezo stack described in TLens-like terms.

**2025-01-01 → 2025-12-09: Qualification, doubt and exits**  
Jason Hartlove opened SPIE 2025 for Meta (2025-01); the CML-citing wearable camera was allowed 2025-01-21 and abandoned 2025-05-08. Huang left in April 2025 with a profile saying the piezo module passed 'full reliability qualifications for AR/VR/MR'; a forum claimed mass production that same month (rumor). Reality Labs' 2025-04-17 paper argued against autofocus in all-day glasses; the wide-FOV application took a final rejection on 2025-07-10; a new LC/electrowetting 'tunable lens camera module for AR glasses' was filed 2025-11-14; US 12,493,197 (macro AF, citing CML and poLight) was granted 2025-12-09. The record is strongest for a VR/MR camera and weakest for all-day glasses.

**2026-02-23 → 2027-12-31: Grants cluster before launch**  
Allowance of the wide-FOV application (2026-02-23) was followed by a barrel-via continuation (2026-04-30), US 12,631,847 'lens barrel with an integrated tunable lens' (2026-05-19, expiring 2044-05-23) and US 12,663,619 (2026-06-23, 40.4 months after filing, citing three poLight documents); the continuation published 2026-09-17, six days before Connect. Patrick Llull joined from Apple as sensor-to-display architect (May 2026) and October 2026 job posts seek a camera-IQ lead for AI glasses/VR passthrough and a camera-system engineer owning modules 'through qualification and mass-production ramp', 146 days before the VR Glasses window. Further grants are possible Q4 2026-2027.

### Analysts, brokers & press (supplier; 16 dated signals)

Market-sizing and shipment claims from Omdia, which puts Meta at 7.4m AI glasses in 2025 and 3.4m (81% share) in 1H26. These volumes set the scale of any Meta design-win for an autofocus supplier, even though the mainstream glasses remain fixed-focus. Only Analyst Group (commissioned, NOK 9.0/share) formally covers poLight. DBS covers Q Tech and cites 'low-power tunable lenses' as a smart-glasses opportunity but, in the accessible notes, never names poLight, Meta or 2027; the alleged DBS 'H1 2027' Meta/Q Tech/poLight claim remains unverified. Press and analyst commentary relevant to the SMA-vs-TLens comparison: Karl Guttag's CES 2023 power figures (CML ~64 mW moving vs TLens <6 mW), Yole's read of the Alps licence, and Everbright's sizing of the TDK→Q Tech deal. Analyst material in this file is limited to Patsnap's 2026-09-17 liquid-lens AF actuator patent landscape (LG Innotek 7, Corning 5, Meta 3, Apple 3; filings down 67% 2021-2024). Analyst, broker and press claims: DBS's Aug-2026 Q Tech note publicly cites "low-power tunable lenses" in smart glasses, while the Meta/1H27 mass-production sentence attributed to it is gated and unverified; DBS on Sunny flags an FY27 smart-glasses ramp; Counterpoint and Everbright add manufacturing-share and Cowell colour. Press and analyst claims: KGOnTech's March 2023 CES/PW report placed TLens in the Magic Leap 2 and Sharp's CES-2023 VR prototype at <6 mW, an early confirmed-in-product data point for poLight in head-worn devices. Analyst, broker, market-data and in-thread assistant claims: DBS Research (26 Aug 2026) is the only source naming a Meta + Q Tech + poLight project with 1H27 mass production; Omdia, Counterpoint and TrendForce supply market sizing; the GPT-6 Pro assistant's probability estimates, rankings and valuation models are logged here.

**2023-03-04 → 2025-05-13: Technology comparisons**  
Karl Guttag's CES 2023 notes set CML's 8-wire SMA (~3.2 V/20 mA) against TLens's '<6 mW' (2023-03-04); Yole inferred a CML licence behind Alps's SMA production (2023-04-14); the VR market was 6.9m units in 2024 with Meta at ~77%; Everbright sized the TDK deal at 2,000+ patents and 2,000+ employees (2025-05-13). Nobody yet connected these actuators to a dated glasses launch.

**2026-02-06 → 2026-08-28: Sell-side turns to glasses**  
A commissioned initiation valued poLight at NOK 9.0 (2026-02-06); Omdia counted 7.4m Meta AI glasses in 2025 (+281%) and >15m forecast for 2026 (2026-03-05); DBS's Meta note of 2026-05-08 never mentioned poLight. Within three days DBS published BUY on Q Tech (2026-08-26, TP HKD 12.60, 'low-power tunable lenses') and on Sunny Optical (2026-08-28, 'FY27 ramp of major smart-glasses programmes'); the Meta/Q Tech/poLight '1H27' sentence attributed to the gated Q Tech PDF the same day remains unverified. Everbright put Cowell's glasses mass production in 2026-2027.

**2026-09-17 → 2027-12-31: Secondary and AI-assistant noise**  
Patsnap's liquid-lens landscape (2026-09-17) counts LG Innotek 7, Corning 5, Meta 3 and Apple 3 patents; Omdia put Meta at 3.4m AI glasses in 1H26 with 81.3% share (2026-09-22). The 2026-09-25 cluster of assistant outputs (62% TLens-in-VR-Glasses, 70% U.S.-OEM-is-Meta, a first production PO of NOK 5-15m by late Q4 2026/early Q1 2027, ~400k units year one) are model estimates, not sourced evidence, and one was retracted as 'too confident'. Omdia and TrendForce expect the XR market to shift to glasses from 2027, the backdrop every supplier is positioning for.

## 6. What the earlier ChatGPT research thread established

The thread tests one thesis: that poLight's TLens piezo-MEMS tunable lens is the autofocus element in the stereo passthrough cameras of Meta's ~100 g 'Meta VR Glasses', announced at Connect on 23 September 2026 for a spring-2027 launch at USD 1,299.99. Meta's developer specifications confirm 'color stereoscopic (26 PPD) passthrough with autofocus and depth sensing' but name no actuator or supplier, so the question is purely supplier identity.

Strongest evidence for poLight: Meta's granted patent US 12,663,619 (granted 23 June 2026) claims a flexible membrane, hard substrate, deformable polymer and thin-film PZT actuators - essentially TLens's architecture - and cites three 2022 poLight documents; a documented chain in which an anonymous top-tier U.S. OEM that had qualified TLens for years backed Q Technology's ~NOK 171m investment and dedicated TLens line (April 2025), followed by qualification POs (Aug 2025, Feb 2026, Jun 2026) for 'very advanced, high-end glasses' that could 'ramp in huge volumes'; Q Tech's 2024 'US M brand' XR vendor code; DBS Research (26 Aug 2026) stating a Meta project combining Q Tech modules with poLight's tunable lens enters mass production in 1H27; and a former Meta engineer's LinkedIn profile citing a 'miniature piezoelectric tunable lens AF camera module' that passed AR/VR/MR reliability qualification.

Strongest counter-evidence: poLight has never named Meta; its CEO confirmed on 19 Aug 2026 that the three headline programs are different end customers, so clues cannot be stacked; the 2026 design-in targets a 2026 product; the Q Tech program is still 'qualification' with only ~NOK 2.5m of attributable orders; DBS gives no source and disclaims verification; Meta's patents could describe an in-house or third-party PZT lens; CML, Optotune and VCM makers cannot be excluded.

Final probability: estimates oscillated 30-50% -> 62% -> 68% -> 78% -> 65% -> 88% -> 85% before settling at ~70% (60-65% without DBS) that TLens is in the spring-2027 Meta VR Glasses, with ~90% that Meta is the Q Tech-backed OEM. Not covered: teardowns or any direct Meta/poLight/Q Tech confirmation, full USPTO file wrappers, CML's latest accounts, Q Tech primary filings linking the 2024 M-brand customer to the 2025 investment, poLight's cumulative 2026 order total, actual Meta volumes or camera power figures, and the LinkedIn individual's identity.
